I mean… yeah, I think so. Guilty, definitely, or at least really uncomfortable with myself afterward. It’s not like I finish eating and feel satisfied, you know? It’s more like I kind of snap out of it and start replaying what I ate, and I feel embarrassed even though nobody else saw it.

I don’t really like saying that, but yeah. I’ll be sitting there thinking, “What is wrong with me? Why couldn’t I just stop after the meal?” And then I don’t want to talk to anyone or have anyone ask what I had for dinner or anything, because it feels stupid and gross to explain.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating more than you planned happen?\n\nPatient: Most weeks, it’s at least once. Sometimes it feels like I can go a few days and be okay, and then there’ll be one night where it just happens again—usually after work or when I’m already tired and stressed—and I eat way more than I meant to.\n\nAnd then… I usually try to make up for it somehow. Like, sometimes I’ll make myself throw up, or I’ll take laxatives, or I’ll tell myself I’m just not going to eat the next day, or I’ll work out harder than I normally would. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Most weeks it’s at least once, I’d say. Like there’ll be one night where I eat way more than I meant to, and then afterward I get really freaked out about it and I feel like I have to undo it somehow.

Sometimes that means I make myself throw up, sometimes I take laxatives, or I’ll tell myself I’m just going to skip meals the next day. Other times I’ll go work out harder than I normally would, even if I’m tired, because I feel like I have to balance it out. It’s not the exact same thing every time, but it’s that same pattern.

And it’s been going on for several months now. I kept thinking it was just a bad week or stress from work or something, but it hasn’t really stopped.

Yeah… that does happen too. I think more often than I want to admit, honestly. Like, I’ll get off a long shift at the shop, or sometimes after I’ve been trying to study or get other stuff done, and I’ll tell myself I’m just going to grab something quick. So I guess the answer is… yeah, I have episodes where I eat way more than I planned and then don’t really do anything to compensate. It’s usually after work or when I’m worn down, and it all happens in one sitting before I really feel like I’ve caught up with myself.

Why couldn’t I just stop after the meal?” And then I don’t want to talk to anyone or have anyone ask what I had for dinner or anything, because it feels stupid and gross to explain.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating more than you planned happen?\n\nPatient: Most weeks, it’s at least once. Sometimes it feels like I can go a few days and be okay, and then there’ll be one night where it just happens again—usually after work or when I’m already tired and stressed—and I eat way more than I meant to.\n\nAnd then… I usually try to make up for it somehow. Like, sometimes I’ll make myself throw up, or I’ll take laxatives, or I’ll tell myself I’m just not going to eat the next day, or I’ll work out harder than I normally would. It’s not every single day or anything, but it’s been going on for several months now, and most weeks there’s at least one night like that.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up, take laxatives, or do other things to try to make up for eating more than you planned?\n\nPatient: Most weeks it’s at least once, I’d say. Like there’ll be one night where I eat way more than I meant to, and then afterward I get really freaked out about it and I feel like I have to undo it somehow.\n\nSometimes that means I make myself throw up, sometimes I take laxatives, or I’ll tell myself I’m just going to skip meals the next day. Other times I’ll go work out harder than I normally would, even if I’m tired, because I feel like I have to balance it out. It’s not the exact same thing every time, but it’s that same pattern.\n\nAnd it’s been going on for several months now. I kept thinking it was just a bad week or stress from work or something, but it hasn’t really stopped.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. I mean, I check sometimes, but not in a very organized way, and my weight kind of goes up and down a little depending on the week, or what I ate, or how much I’ve been working and sweating at the shop. So I don’t know if I’d call it “significant.”\n\nMy clothes don’t feel dramatically different or anything like that. Sometimes I feel like I look bigger, but then I also know I’m not always seeing it clearly, if that makes sense. I haven’t had some big, obvious change where people are commenting on it. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes where you eat more than you planned without then trying to make up for it, such as by making yourself throw up, taking laxatives, or exercising extra?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that does happen too. I think more often than I want to admit, honestly. Like, I’ll get off a long shift at the shop, or sometimes after I’ve been trying to study or get other stuff done, and I’ll tell myself I’m just going to grab something quick. But then it turns into a full fast-food meal, and then snacks, and then I’m still going through whatever’s in the fridge when I get home.\n\nSometimes after that I do try to make up for it, like I said, but not every single time. There are times where I’m just so tired or kind of numb that I don’t do anything afterward. I just sit there feeling gross and frustrated with myself, but I don’t throw up or take anything or go exercise extra. I just go to bed or try to ignore it.\n\nSo I guess the answer is… yeah, I have episodes where I eat way more than I planned and then don’t really do anything to compensate. It’s usually after work or when I’m worn down, and it all happens in one sitting before I really feel like I’ve caught up with myself.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’d say… probably a few times a week. Like, not every single night, but enough that it’s become this regular thing I’m worried about.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… that’s probably the part that scares me the most, honestly. Because it doesn’t feel like normal overeating, like, “Oh, I had an extra snack.” It feels like once I start on those nights, something kind of takes over.

I’ll be eating and I’ll already be thinking, “Stop, you’re going to feel sick, you don’t even really want this anymore,” but my hands just keep grabbing more food. Like I’ll finish one thing and immediately be opening something else, even while I’m telling myself not to. And sometimes I’m already uncomfortable, like my stomach hurts or I feel kind of nauseous, but I still keep going.

So yeah, I do feel like I can’t really control it in the moment. It’s not like I’m calmly deciding to eat that much. It’s more like I notice what I’m doing while I’m doing it, but I can’t make myself stop until I’m way past the point where I wanted to.

It’s been happening enough that it’s starting to worry me, and I didn’t really know who else to talk to about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating more than you planned?\n\nPatient: I mean… yeah, I think so. Guilty, definitely, or at least really uncomfortable with myself afterward. It’s not like I finish eating and feel satisfied, you know? It’s more like I kind of snap out of it and start replaying what I ate, and I feel embarrassed even though nobody else saw it.\n\nAshamed is maybe the right word too, I guess. I don’t really like saying that, but yeah. I’ll be sitting there thinking, “What is wrong with me? Why couldn’t I just stop after the meal?” And then I don’t want to talk to anyone or have anyone ask what I had for dinner or anything, because it feels stupid and gross to explain.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating more than you planned happen?\n\nPatient: Most weeks, it’s at least once. Sometimes it feels like I can go a few days and be okay, and then there’ll be one night where it just happens again—usually after work or when I’m already tired and stressed—and I eat way more than I meant to.\n\nAnd then… I usually try to make up for it somehow. Like, sometimes I’ll make myself throw up, or I’ll take laxatives, or I’ll tell myself I’m just not going to eat the next day, or I’ll work out harder than I normally would. It’s not every single day or anything, but it’s been going on for several months now, and most weeks there’s at least one night like that.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up, take laxatives, or do other things to try to make up for eating more than you planned?\n\nPatient: Most weeks it’s at least once, I’d say. Like there’ll be one night where I eat way more than I meant to, and then afterward I get really freaked out about it and I feel like I have to undo it somehow.\n\nSometimes that means I make myself throw up, sometimes I take laxatives, or I’ll tell myself I’m just going to skip meals the next day. Other times I’ll go work out harder than I normally would, even if I’m tired, because I feel like I have to balance it out. It’s not the exact same thing every time, but it’s that same pattern.\n\nAnd it’s been going on for several months now. I kept thinking it was just a bad week or stress from work or something, but it hasn’t really stopped.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. I mean, I check sometimes, but not in a very organized way, and my weight kind of goes up and down a little depending on the week, or what I ate, or how much I’ve been working and sweating at the shop. So I don’t know if I’d call it “significant.”\n\nMy clothes don’t feel dramatically different or anything like that. Sometimes I feel like I look bigger, but then I also know I’m not always seeing it clearly, if that makes sense. I haven’t had some big, obvious change where people are commenting on it. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes where you eat more than you planned without then trying to make up for it, such as by making yourself throw up, taking laxatives, or exercising extra?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that does happen too. I think more often than I want to admit, honestly. Like, I’ll get off a long shift at the shop, or sometimes after I’ve been trying to study or get other stuff done, and I’ll tell myself I’m just going to grab something quick. But then it turns into a full fast-food meal, and then snacks, and then I’m still going through whatever’s in the fridge when I get home.\n\nSometimes after that I do try to make up for it, like I said, but not every single time. There are times where I’m just so tired or kind of numb that I don’t do anything afterward. I just sit there feeling gross and frustrated with myself, but I don’t throw up or take anything or go exercise extra. I just go to bed or try to ignore it.\n\nSo I guess the answer is… yeah, I have episodes where I eat way more than I planned and then don’t really do anything to compensate. It’s usually after work or when I’m worn down, and it all happens in one sitting before I really feel like I’ve caught up with myself.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes where you eat more than you planned without then trying to make up for it?\n\nPatient: It’s kind of the reason I came in, honestly. Lately I’ll get off a long shift, or I’ll finish trying to study, and I’ll be telling myself I’m just going to eat something normal, just something quick. But then it turns into way more than I meant it to.\n\nLike I’ll get a full fast-food meal, and then I’ll still have snacks after that, and then when I get home I’ll end up looking through the fridge and eating whatever’s there. And it’s not spread out over a whole day, it’s more like all in one sitting, like once it starts I have a hard time stopping or even slowing down.\n\nAs far as how often… I don’t know the exact number, but it’s been happening pretty regularly lately. Not every day, but enough that it feels like a pattern and not just, you know, one bad night.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you have episodes where you eat more than you planned and then don't try to make up for it?\n\nPatient: I’d say… probably a few times a week. Like, not every single night, but enough that it’s become this regular thing I’m worried about.\n\nIt usually happens after a long shift or when I’ve been studying and I’m already tired, and I’ll think I’m just going to grab dinner, something quick. Then it turns into a full fast-food meal, then snacks, and then when I get home I’m still eating whatever’s in the fridge. And when I don’t try to make up for it afterward, it’s usually because I’m just worn out or kind of checked out. So, yeah… maybe two or three times a week, sometimes more if it’s been a rough week.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes where you eat more than you planned, do you feel like you're unable to stop yourself or control how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s probably the part that scares me the most, honestly. Because it doesn’t feel like normal overeating, like, “Oh, I had an extra snack.” It feels like once I start on those nights, something kind of takes over.\n\nI’ll be eating and I’ll already be thinking, “Stop, you’re going to feel sick, you don’t even really want this anymore,” but my hands just keep grabbing more food. Like I’ll finish one thing and immediately be opening something else, even while I’m telling myself not to. And sometimes I’m already uncomfortable, like my stomach hurts or I feel kind of nauseous, but I still keep going.\n\nSo yeah, I do feel like I can’t really control it in the moment. It’s not like I’m calmly deciding to eat that much. It’s more like I notice what I’m doing while I’m doing it, but I can’t make myself stop until I’m way past the point where I wanted to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’m not totally sure, honestly. I mean… sometimes, yeah, I’m probably tired or wound up from the day, but I don’t always notice a clear “I’m anxious, so now I’m eating” kind of thing.

Like I’ll finish one thing and immediately be opening something else, even while I’m telling myself not to. And sometimes I’m already uncomfortable, like my stomach hurts or I feel kind of nauseous, but I still keep going.\n\nSo yeah, I do feel like I can’t really control it in the moment. Sometimes it feels like I can go a few days and be okay, and then there’ll be one night where it just happens again—usually after work or when I’m already tired and stressed—and I eat way more than I meant to.\n\nAnd then… I usually try to make up for it somehow. Like, sometimes I’ll make myself throw up, or I’ll take laxatives, or I’ll tell myself I’m just not going to eat the next day, or I’ll work out harder than I normally would. It’s not every single day or anything, but it’s been going on for several months now, and most weeks there’s at least one night like that.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up, take laxatives, or do other things to try to make up for eating more than you planned?\n\nPatient: Most weeks it’s at least once, I’d say. Like there’ll be one night where I eat way more than I meant to, and then afterward I get really freaked out about it and I feel like I have to undo it somehow.\n\nSometimes that means I make myself throw up, sometimes I take laxatives, or I’ll tell myself I’m just going to skip meals the next day. Other times I’ll go work out harder than I normally would, even if I’m tired, because I feel like I have to balance it out. It’s not the exact same thing every time, but it’s that same pattern.\n\nAnd it’s been going on for several months now. I kept thinking it was just a bad week or stress from work or something, but it hasn’t really stopped.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. I mean, I check sometimes, but not in a very organized way, and my weight kind of goes up and down a little depending on the week, or what I ate, or how much I’ve been working and sweating at the shop. So I don’t know if I’d call it “significant.”\n\nMy clothes don’t feel dramatically different or anything like that. Sometimes I feel like I look bigger, but then I also know I’m not always seeing it clearly, if that makes sense. I haven’t had some big, obvious change where people are commenting on it. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes where you eat more than you planned without then trying to make up for it, such as by making yourself throw up, taking laxatives, or exercising extra?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that does happen too. I think more often than I want to admit, honestly. Like, I’ll get off a long shift at the shop, or sometimes after I’ve been trying to study or get other stuff done, and I’ll tell myself I’m just going to grab something quick. But then it turns into a full fast-food meal, and then snacks, and then I’m still going through whatever’s in the fridge when I get home.\n\nSometimes after that I do try to make up for it, like I said, but not every single time. There are times where I’m just so tired or kind of numb that I don’t do anything afterward. I just sit there feeling gross and frustrated with myself, but I don’t throw up or take anything or go exercise extra. I just go to bed or try to ignore it.\n\nSo I guess the answer is… yeah, I have episodes where I eat way more than I planned and then don’t really do anything to compensate. It’s usually after work or when I’m worn down, and it all happens in one sitting before I really feel like I’ve caught up with myself.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes where you eat more than you planned without then trying to make up for it?\n\nPatient: It’s kind of the reason I came in, honestly. Lately I’ll get off a long shift, or I’ll finish trying to study, and I’ll be telling myself I’m just going to eat something normal, just something quick. But then it turns into way more than I meant it to.\n\nLike I’ll get a full fast-food meal, and then I’ll still have snacks after that, and then when I get home I’ll end up looking through the fridge and eating whatever’s there. And it’s not spread out over a whole day, it’s more like all in one sitting, like once it starts I have a hard time stopping or even slowing down.\n\nAs far as how often… I don’t know the exact number, but it’s been happening pretty regularly lately. Not every day, but enough that it feels like a pattern and not just, you know, one bad night.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you have episodes where you eat more than you planned and then don't try to make up for it?\n\nPatient: I’d say… probably a few times a week. Like, not every single night, but enough that it’s become this regular thing I’m worried about.\n\nIt usually happens after a long shift or when I’ve been studying and I’m already tired, and I’ll think I’m just going to grab dinner, something quick. Then it turns into a full fast-food meal, then snacks, and then when I get home I’m still eating whatever’s in the fridge. And when I don’t try to make up for it afterward, it’s usually because I’m just worn out or kind of checked out. So, yeah… maybe two or three times a week, sometimes more if it’s been a rough week.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes where you eat more than you planned, do you feel like you're unable to stop yourself or control how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s probably the part that scares me the most, honestly. Because it doesn’t feel like normal overeating, like, “Oh, I had an extra snack.” It feels like once I start on those nights, something kind of takes over.\n\nI’ll be eating and I’ll already be thinking, “Stop, you’re going to feel sick, you don’t even really want this anymore,” but my hands just keep grabbing more food. Like I’ll finish one thing and immediately be opening something else, even while I’m telling myself not to. And sometimes I’m already uncomfortable, like my stomach hurts or I feel kind of nauseous, but I still keep going.\n\nSo yeah, I do feel like I can’t really control it in the moment. It’s not like I’m calmly deciding to eat that much. It’s more like I notice what I’m doing while I’m doing it, but I can’t make myself stop until I’m way past the point where I wanted to.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel anxious or stressed right before these episodes where you eat more than you planned and feel like you can't stop?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. I mean… sometimes, yeah, I’m probably tired or wound up from the day, but I don’t always notice a clear “I’m anxious, so now I’m eating” kind of thing.\n\nA lot of the time it’s more like I’m on autopilot. I’ll get home or stop for food, and I don’t really check in with myself until I’m already in the middle of it. Looking back, I might think, “Okay, maybe I was stressed,” but in the moment it doesn’t always feel obvious. It’s not like every time I can point to one thing and say, “That caused it.”\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I haven’t really paid close enough attention to know for sure. I know there’s usually something going on in the background, like being worn down, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious or stressed every time.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you make yourself throw up, take laxatives, or do other things to try to make up for eating more than you planned?\n\nPatient: Most weeks it’s at least once… sometimes more, but I’d say there’s usually at least one night where I eat like that and then I try to “fix” it somehow.\n\nThrowing up is one of the things I’ve done, yeah. And I’ve used laxatives sometimes too, or I’ll tell myself I’m just not going to eat the next day, or I’ll work out way more than I normally would to try to make up for it. It kind of depends on the night and how bad I feel afterward, I guess.\n\nIt’s not like it happened once or twice and then stopped. This has been going on for several months now, where most weeks there’s at least one time I do something afterward because I feel like I have to undo it somehow. I know that probably sounds… I don’t know. It’s embarrassing to say out loud.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I’ll get home or stop for food, and I don’t really check in with myself until I’m already in the middle of it. Looking back, I might think, “Okay, maybe I was stressed,” but in the moment it doesn’t always feel obvious. It’s not like every time I can point to one thing and say, “That caused it.”\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I haven’t really paid close enough attention to know for sure. I know there’s usually something going on in the background, like being worn down, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious or stressed every time.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you make yourself throw up, take laxatives, or do other things to try to make up for eating more than you planned?\n\nPatient: Most weeks it’s at least once… sometimes more, but I’d say there’s usually at least one night where I eat like that and then I try to “fix” it somehow.\n\nThrowing up is one of the things I’ve done, yeah. And I’ve used laxatives sometimes too, or I’ll tell myself I’m just not going to eat the next day, or I’ll work out way more than I normally would to try to make up for it. It kind of depends on the night and how bad I feel afterward, I guess.\n\nIt’s not like it happened once or twice and then stopped. This has been going on for several months now, where most weeks there’s at least one time I do something afterward because I feel like I have to undo it somehow. I know that probably sounds… I don’t know. It’s embarrassing to say out loud.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you make yourself throw up, take laxatives, or do other things to try to make up for eating more than you planned every time it happens?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 26 / bus_or_truck_mechanic_or_diesel_engine_specialist / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Compensatory_Behavior_Purging\nmanifestation: \"Sometimes after I eat too much, I go to the bathroom and make myself throw up, or I take laxatives because I’m scared of what the food is going to do to me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not every single time, no. Sometimes I just feel awful and sit with it, or I tell myself I’ll somehow “make up for it” later. But… a lot of the time, if I feel like I really overdid it, I panic about what the food is going to do to me.

That’s when I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. Or sometimes I’ll take laxatives, because in my head it feels like I need to get it out of me somehow. I know that sounds messed up, but in the moment it feels like this urgent thing, like I can’t just let the food stay there.

So, no, not every time exactly… but it happens enough that it’s not rare. It’s one of the main things I do after I eat too much.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s not every time I overeat, but it’s… often enough that I’m worried about saying the number out loud. Like, if I have one of those nights where I feel like I really lost control, then a lot of the time I end up going to the bathroom afterward and making myself throw up.

I don’t always plan it ahead of time. Sometimes I’ll be sitting there feeling full and disgusting and scared about what I just ate, and then it turns into this thing where I feel like I have to get it out of me. So I’ll throw up, or if I don’t do that, sometimes I take laxatives instead because I’m still scared of what the food is going to do to me.

If you’re asking specifically how often I throw up, I’d say… some weeks maybe once, sometimes more if I’ve had more of those episodes. It depends on how bad I feel afterward, but it’s definitely not rare.

It’s not the exact same thing every time, but it’s that same pattern.\n\nAnd it’s been going on for several months now. I kept thinking it was just a bad week or stress from work or something, but it hasn’t really stopped.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. I mean, I check sometimes, but not in a very organized way, and my weight kind of goes up and down a little depending on the week, or what I ate, or how much I’ve been working and sweating at the shop. So I don’t know if I’d call it “significant.”\n\nMy clothes don’t feel dramatically different or anything like that. Sometimes I feel like I look bigger, but then I also know I’m not always seeing it clearly, if that makes sense. I haven’t had some big, obvious change where people are commenting on it. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes where you eat more than you planned without then trying to make up for it, such as by making yourself throw up, taking laxatives, or exercising extra?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that does happen too. I think more often than I want to admit, honestly. Like, I’ll get off a long shift at the shop, or sometimes after I’ve been trying to study or get other stuff done, and I’ll tell myself I’m just going to grab something quick. But then it turns into a full fast-food meal, and then snacks, and then I’m still going through whatever’s in the fridge when I get home.\n\nSometimes after that I do try to make up for it, like I said, but not every single time. There are times where I’m just so tired or kind of numb that I don’t do anything afterward. I just sit there feeling gross and frustrated with myself, but I don’t throw up or take anything or go exercise extra. I just go to bed or try to ignore it.\n\nSo I guess the answer is… yeah, I have episodes where I eat way more than I planned and then don’t really do anything to compensate. It’s usually after work or when I’m worn down, and it all happens in one sitting before I really feel like I’ve caught up with myself.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes where you eat more than you planned without then trying to make up for it?\n\nPatient: It’s kind of the reason I came in, honestly. Lately I’ll get off a long shift, or I’ll finish trying to study, and I’ll be telling myself I’m just going to eat something normal, just something quick. But then it turns into way more than I meant it to.\n\nLike I’ll get a full fast-food meal, and then I’ll still have snacks after that, and then when I get home I’ll end up looking through the fridge and eating whatever’s there. And it’s not spread out over a whole day, it’s more like all in one sitting, like once it starts I have a hard time stopping or even slowing down.\n\nAs far as how often… I don’t know the exact number, but it’s been happening pretty regularly lately. Not every day, but enough that it feels like a pattern and not just, you know, one bad night.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you have episodes where you eat more than you planned and then don't try to make up for it?\n\nPatient: I’d say… probably a few times a week. Like, not every single night, but enough that it’s become this regular thing I’m worried about.\n\nIt usually happens after a long shift or when I’ve been studying and I’m already tired, and I’ll think I’m just going to grab dinner, something quick. Then it turns into a full fast-food meal, then snacks, and then when I get home I’m still eating whatever’s in the fridge. And when I don’t try to make up for it afterward, it’s usually because I’m just worn out or kind of checked out. So, yeah… maybe two or three times a week, sometimes more if it’s been a rough week.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes where you eat more than you planned, do you feel like you're unable to stop yourself or control how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s probably the part that scares me the most, honestly. Because it doesn’t feel like normal overeating, like, “Oh, I had an extra snack.” It feels like once I start on those nights, something kind of takes over.\n\nI’ll be eating and I’ll already be thinking, “Stop, you’re going to feel sick, you don’t even really want this anymore,” but my hands just keep grabbing more food. Like I’ll finish one thing and immediately be opening something else, even while I’m telling myself not to. And sometimes I’m already uncomfortable, like my stomach hurts or I feel kind of nauseous, but I still keep going.\n\nSo yeah, I do feel like I can’t really control it in the moment. It’s not like I’m calmly deciding to eat that much. It’s more like I notice what I’m doing while I’m doing it, but I can’t make myself stop until I’m way past the point where I wanted to.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel anxious or stressed right before these episodes where you eat more than you planned and feel like you can't stop?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. I mean… sometimes, yeah, I’m probably tired or wound up from the day, but I don’t always notice a clear “I’m anxious, so now I’m eating” kind of thing.\n\nA lot of the time it’s more like I’m on autopilot. I’ll get home or stop for food, and I don’t really check in with myself until I’m already in the middle of it. Looking back, I might think, “Okay, maybe I was stressed,” but in the moment it doesn’t always feel obvious. It’s not like every time I can point to one thing and say, “That caused it.”\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I haven’t really paid close enough attention to know for sure. I know there’s usually something going on in the background, like being worn down, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious or stressed every time.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you make yourself throw up, take laxatives, or do other things to try to make up for eating more than you planned?\n\nPatient: Most weeks it’s at least once… sometimes more, but I’d say there’s usually at least one night where I eat like that and then I try to “fix” it somehow.\n\nThrowing up is one of the things I’ve done, yeah. And I’ve used laxatives sometimes too, or I’ll tell myself I’m just not going to eat the next day, or I’ll work out way more than I normally would to try to make up for it. It kind of depends on the night and how bad I feel afterward, I guess.\n\nIt’s not like it happened once or twice and then stopped. This has been going on for several months now, where most weeks there’s at least one time I do something afterward because I feel like I have to undo it somehow. I know that probably sounds… I don’t know. It’s embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up, take laxatives, or do other things to try to make up for eating more than you planned every time it happens?\n\nPatient: Not every single time, no. But… a lot of the time, if I feel like I really overdid it, I panic about what the food is going to do to me.\n\nThat’s when I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. Or sometimes I’ll take laxatives, because in my head it feels like I need to get it out of me somehow. I know that sounds messed up, but in the moment it feels like this urgent thing, like I can’t just let the food stay there.\n\nSo, no, not every time exactly… but it happens enough that it’s not rare. It’s one of the main things I do after I eat too much.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating more than you planned?\n\nPatient: It’s not every time I overeat, but it’s… often enough that I’m worried about saying the number out loud. Like, if I have one of those nights where I feel like I really lost control, then a lot of the time I end up going to the bathroom afterward and making myself throw up.\n\nI don’t always plan it ahead of time. Sometimes I’ll be sitting there feeling full and disgusting and scared about what I just ate, and then it turns into this thing where I feel like I have to get it out of me. So I’ll throw up, or if I don’t do that, sometimes I take laxatives instead because I’m still scared of what the food is going to do to me.\n\nIf you’re asking specifically how often I throw up, I’d say… some weeks maybe once, sometimes more if I’ve had more of those episodes. But then it turns into way more than I meant it to.\n\nLike I’ll get a full fast-food meal, and then I’ll still have snacks after that, and then when I get home I’ll end up looking through the fridge and eating whatever’s there. Not every day, but enough that it feels like a pattern and not just, you know, one bad night.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you have episodes where you eat more than you planned and then don't try to make up for it?\n\nPatient: I’d say… probably a few times a week. Like, not every single night, but enough that it’s become this regular thing I’m worried about.\n\nIt usually happens after a long shift or when I’ve been studying and I’m already tired, and I’ll think I’m just going to grab dinner, something quick. Then it turns into a full fast-food meal, then snacks, and then when I get home I’m still eating whatever’s in the fridge. And when I don’t try to make up for it afterward, it’s usually because I’m just worn out or kind of checked out. So, yeah… maybe two or three times a week, sometimes more if it’s been a rough week.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes where you eat more than you planned, do you feel like you're unable to stop yourself or control how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s probably the part that scares me the most, honestly. Because it doesn’t feel like normal overeating, like, “Oh, I had an extra snack.” It feels like once I start on those nights, something kind of takes over.\n\nI’ll be eating and I’ll already be thinking, “Stop, you’re going to feel sick, you don’t even really want this anymore,” but my hands just keep grabbing more food. Like I’ll finish one thing and immediately be opening something else, even while I’m telling myself not to. And sometimes I’m already uncomfortable, like my stomach hurts or I feel kind of nauseous, but I still keep going.\n\nSo yeah, I do feel like I can’t really control it in the moment. It’s not like I’m calmly deciding to eat that much. It’s more like I notice what I’m doing while I’m doing it, but I can’t make myself stop until I’m way past the point where I wanted to.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel anxious or stressed right before these episodes where you eat more than you planned and feel like you can't stop?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. I mean… sometimes, yeah, I’m probably tired or wound up from the day, but I don’t always notice a clear “I’m anxious, so now I’m eating” kind of thing.\n\nA lot of the time it’s more like I’m on autopilot. I’ll get home or stop for food, and I don’t really check in with myself until I’m already in the middle of it. Looking back, I might think, “Okay, maybe I was stressed,” but in the moment it doesn’t always feel obvious. It’s not like every time I can point to one thing and say, “That caused it.”\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I haven’t really paid close enough attention to know for sure. I know there’s usually something going on in the background, like being worn down, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious or stressed every time.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you make yourself throw up, take laxatives, or do other things to try to make up for eating more than you planned?\n\nPatient: Most weeks it’s at least once… sometimes more, but I’d say there’s usually at least one night where I eat like that and then I try to “fix” it somehow.\n\nThrowing up is one of the things I’ve done, yeah. And I’ve used laxatives sometimes too, or I’ll tell myself I’m just not going to eat the next day, or I’ll work out way more than I normally would to try to make up for it. It kind of depends on the night and how bad I feel afterward, I guess.\n\nIt’s not like it happened once or twice and then stopped. This has been going on for several months now, where most weeks there’s at least one time I do something afterward because I feel like I have to undo it somehow. I know that probably sounds… I don’t know. It’s embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up, take laxatives, or do other things to try to make up for eating more than you planned every time it happens?\n\nPatient: Not every single time, no. Sometimes I just feel awful and sit with it, or I tell myself I’ll somehow “make up for it” later. But… a lot of the time, if I feel like I really overdid it, I panic about what the food is going to do to me.\n\nThat’s when I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. Or sometimes I’ll take laxatives, because in my head it feels like I need to get it out of me somehow. I know that sounds messed up, but in the moment it feels like this urgent thing, like I can’t just let the food stay there.\n\nSo, no, not every time exactly… but it happens enough that it’s not rare. It’s one of the main things I do after I eat too much.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating more than you planned?\n\nPatient: It’s not every time I overeat, but it’s… often enough that I’m worried about saying the number out loud. Like, if I have one of those nights where I feel like I really lost control, then a lot of the time I end up going to the bathroom afterward and making myself throw up.\n\nI don’t always plan it ahead of time. Sometimes I’ll be sitting there feeling full and disgusting and scared about what I just ate, and then it turns into this thing where I feel like I have to get it out of me. So I’ll throw up, or if I don’t do that, sometimes I take laxatives instead because I’m still scared of what the food is going to do to me.\n\nIf you’re asking specifically how often I throw up, I’d say… some weeks maybe once, sometimes more if I’ve had more of those episodes. It depends on how bad I feel afterward, but it’s definitely not rare.\n\nDoctor: Do you throw up after eating more than you planned at least once a week?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most weeks, yes. Not every single time I eat more than I meant to, but at least once a week I usually end up doing it.\n\nIt’s hard to admit, but if I feel like I ate too much, I’ll get really scared about what the food is going to do to me, and I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. Sometimes if I don’t throw up, I take laxatives instead, but throwing up is definitely one of the main things I do after. It feels like I’m trying to undo it, even though I know that’s not really… I don’t know. It’s embarrassing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
